def main():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.read().split()
    m = int(input[0])
    n = int(input[1])
    grid = input[2:2+m]
    rows = m
    cols = len(grid[0]) if m > 0 else 0

    # Preprocess blocks
    visited = [[False for _ in range(cols)] for _ in range(rows)]
    block_map = [[None for _ in range(cols)] for _ in range(rows)]
    blocks = []
    for y in range(rows):
        for x in range(cols):
            if not visited[y][x] and grid[y][x] != '0':
                color = grid[y][x]
                # Determine y_end
                y_end = y
                while y_end + 1 < rows and grid[y_end + 1][x] == color:
                    y_end += 1
                # Determine x_end
                x_end = x
                while x_end + 1 < cols:
                    valid = True
                    for yy in range(y, y_end + 1):
                        if grid[yy][x_end + 1] != color:
                            valid = False
                            break
                    if valid:
                        x_end += 1
                    else:
                        break
                # Create block tuple (min_x, max_x, min_y, max_y, color)
                block = (x, x_end, y, y_end, color)
                blocks.append(block)
                # Mark visited and block_map
                for xx in range(x, x_end + 1):
                    for yy in range(y, y_end + 1):
                        visited[yy][xx] = True
                        block_map[yy][xx] = block

    # Initial state
    initial_block = block_map[0][0]
    current_block = initial_block
    dp = (1, 0)  # right
    cp = 'left'
    steps_done = 0
    states = {}
    found_cycle = False

    def rotate_clockwise(direction):
        dx, dy = direction
        if dx == 1:
            return (0, 1)
        elif dy == 1:
            return (-1, 0)
        elif dx == -1:
            return (0, -1)
        else:
            return (1, 0)

    def compute_exit_pixel(block, direction, cp_dir):
        min_x, max_x, min_y, max_y, _ = block
        dx, dy = direction
        if dx == 1:  # right
            x = max_x
            y = min_y if cp_dir == 'left' else max_y
        elif dx == -1:  # left
            x = min_x
            y = max_y if cp_dir == 'left' else min_y
        elif dy == 1:  # down
            y = max_y
            x = max_x if cp_dir == 'left' else min_x
        else:  # up
            y = min_y
            x = min_x if cp_dir == 'left' else max_x
        return (x, y)

    while steps_done < n:
        state_key = (current_block, dp, cp)
        if state_key in states:
            # Cycle detected
            cycle_start = states[state_key]
            cycle_length = steps_done - cycle_start
            remaining = (n - cycle_start) % cycle_length
            # Simulate remaining steps
            for _ in range(remaining):
                exit_x, exit_y = compute_exit_pixel(current_block, dp, cp)
                dx, dy = dp
                new_x = exit_x + dx
                new_y = exit_y + dy
                if 0 <= new_x < cols and 0 <= new_y < rows and grid[new_y][new_x] != '0':
                    current_block = block_map[new_y][new_x]
                else:
                    if cp == 'left':
                        cp = 'right'
                    else:
                        cp = 'left'
                        dp = rotate_clockwise(dp)
                steps_done += 1
            break
        else:
            states[state_key] = steps_done

        # Proceed with the current step
        exit_x, exit_y = compute_exit_pixel(current_block, dp, cp)
        dx, dy = dp
        new_x = exit_x + dx
        new_y = exit_y + dy

        if 0 <= new_x < cols and 0 <= new_y < rows and grid[new_y][new_x] != '0':
            current_block = block_map[new_y][new_x]
        else:
            if cp == 'left':
                cp = 'right'
            else:
                cp = 'left'
                dp = rotate_clockwise(dp)
        steps_done += 1

    print(current_block[4])

if __name__ == "__main__":

Example

Output

+def _strict_run(method, obj_or_data, **kwargs):
+    result = method(obj_or_data, **kwargs)
+    if MA2:  # Make marshmallow 2 schemas behave like marshmallow 3
+        data, errors = result
+        if errors:
+            raise marshmallow.ValidationError(errors, data=data)
+    else:
+        data = result
+
+    return data
+
+
+def schema_load(schema, in_data, **kwargs):
+    return _strict_run(schema.load, in_data, **kwargs)
+
+
+def schema_dump(schema, obj, **kwargs):
+    return _strict_run(schema.dump, obj, **kwargs)

<#sep#>Freighter (or barge) was the term given to any spacecraft that was used to transport freight or cargo (such as parts and supplies). Both legitimate businesspersons and smugglers could be found captaining such transports. Freighters were needed from the time space travel began because of the need for supplies to all reaches of the galaxy. Freighters usually traveled with hyperdrives because people would often pay more for quick delivery.
For obvious reasons, freighters were used mainly for trade. Although very few fought in great battles, many freighters did see action. Smugglers and legitimate traders alike had some tangles now and then, but freighters were often armed and shielded so that they could resist attempts on their cargo.
Smuggling was a very prominent criminal fringe activity in the galaxy that involved the transportation of contraband between planets. To do this, a freighter generally of small size such as the Millennium Falcon, was required to transport the contraband past planetary security forces. Some examples of contraband include spice, blasters and medical supplies.
Smugglers often added upgrades to their ships so that they could beat competitors and outwit planetary security forces. Almost every smugglers vessel had improved light speed and sub-light speed drives for increased speed as well as boosted weapons systems to fight their way out of tough situations. Another of the most prevalent modifications to a smugglers freighter were numerous secret compartments to hide contraband from security checks while legitimate cargo occupied the cargo bays. On the Millennium Falcon, these consisted of removable floor plates.
Some freighters became so heavily modified that the mess of cross wired and non traditional parts prevented starship mechanics from working on them effectively. The smugglers themselves were therefore required to have a detailed knowledge of freighter mechanics and electronics to be able to keep their vessels in working order. Most smugglers preferred it this way as they didn't trust anyone tampering with their prized possessions.
Most smugglers freighters required a crew of more than one and as such most smugglers hired a copilot, such as Chewbacca on the Millennium Falcon or the droid LE-BO2D9 aboard the Outrider. To a smuggler, their freighter was everything; their job, their home, their lives. Many smugglers lived in their freighters as they had no terrestrial home.

import sys
from itertools import combinations

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.read().split()
    ptr = 0
    t = int(input[ptr])
    ptr += 1
    for _ in range(t):
        n, c, q = map(int, input[ptr:ptr+3])
        ptr +=3
        p = list(map(int, input[ptr:ptr+n]))
        ptr +=n
        queries = []
        for __ in range(q):
            i, j = map(int, input[ptr:ptr+2])
            ptr +=2
            queries.append( (i-1, j) )  # converting to 0-based index
        
        # Generate all possible interval combinations with cost sum <=c
        # Each interval is [l, r] with r-l >=1 (since cost >=1), and non-overlapping
        # Use BFS to generate all possible combinations
        from collections import deque
        
        # For BFS, each state is (current pos, remaining cost, intervals)
        # We start at pos 0, remaining cost c, and empty intervals
        # Each step, we can choose to skip current pos, or choose to reverse an interval starting at current pos
        # and then jump to the end of the interval +1
        
        # To avoid overlapping intervals, we track the end of the last interval
        # Modified BFS state: (current pos, remaining cost, list of intervals)
        # But this can be optimized by tracking current pos and remaining cost, and generating intervals accordingly
        # However, for the purposes of generating all possible combinations, we need to track the intervals
        
        # Initialize queue with no intervals
        queue = deque()
        queue.append( (0, c, []) )
        combinations_list = []
        
        while queue:
            pos, remaining, intervals = queue.popleft()
            if pos >=n:
                combinations_list.append(intervals)
                continue
            # Option 1: skip this position, move to pos+1
            queue.append( (pos+1, remaining, intervals.copy()) )
            # Option 2: choose to reverse an interval starting at pos with cost m (m >=1)
            for m in range(1, remaining+1):
                # cost m corresponds to length m+1 (r-l =m)
                end = pos + m
                if end >=n:
                    continue
                # Check that this interval is valid
                new_intervals = intervals.copy()
                new_intervals.append( (pos, end) )
                new_remaining = remaining - m
                queue.append( (end+1, new_remaining, new_intervals) )
        
        # Now apply all combinations and collect unique permutations
        seen = set()
        unique_permutations = []
        original = tuple(p)
        seen.add(original)
        unique_permutations.append(original)
        
        for intervals in combinations_list:
            # Apply the intervals to the original permutation
            tmp = list(p)
            for l, r in intervals:
                # Reverse [l, r]
                while l < r:
                    tmp[l], tmp[r] = tmp[r], tmp[l]
                    l +=1
                    r -=1
            perm = tuple(tmp)
            if perm not in seen:
                seen.add(perm)
                unique_permutations.append(perm)
        
        # Sort the unique permutations lex order
        unique_permutations.sort()
        
        # Answer queries
        total = len(unique_permutations)
        for i, j in queries:
            if j > total:
                print(-1)
            else:
                print(unique_permutations[j-1][i])
                
if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()<#sep#>what was the ratio of the excess of the benefits over their fair value in 2015 to 2014 as of december 31 , 2015 and 2014 , the benefit obligations associated with these plans exceeded the fair value of the plan assets by $ 67 million and $ 51 million , respectively .

Therefore, the answer is 4π divided by sqrt(10 - 2*sqrt(5)), and that's the exact simplified form. So I think that's the answer to put.
</think>To calculate the value of \( f(5) \) for the function \( f(n) = \Gamma \left( \frac{1}{n} \right) \Gamma \left( 1 - \frac{1}{n} \right) \), we use Euler's reflection formula:

\[
\Gamma(z) \Gamma(1 - z) = \frac{\pi}{\sin(\pi z)}
\]

Substituting \( z = \frac{1}{5} \), we get:

\[
f(5) = \Gamma \left( \frac{1}{5} \right) \Gamma \left( 1 - \frac{1}{5} \right) = \frac{\pi}{\sin\left( \frac{\pi}{5} \right)}
\]

The exact value of \( \sin\left( \frac{\pi}{5} \right) \) is:

\[
\sin\left( \frac{\pi}{5} \right) = \frac{\sqrt{10 - 2\sqrt{5}}}{4}
\]

Substituting this into the expression for \( f(5) \):

\[
f(5) = \frac{\pi}{\frac{\sqrt{10 - 2\sqrt{5}}}{4}} = \frac{4\pi}{\sqrt{10 - 2\sqrt{5}}}
\]

To rationalize the denominator, we multiply the numerator and the denominator by \( \sqrt{10 + 2\sqrt{5}} \):

\[
\frac{4\pi}{\sqrt{10 - 2\sqrt{5}}} \cdot \frac{\sqrt{10 + 2\sqrt{5}}}{\sqrt{10 + 2\sqrt{5}}} = \frac{4\pi \sqrt{10 + 2\sqrt{5}}}{\sqrt{(10 - 2\sqrt{5})(10 + 2\sqrt{5})}}
\]

Simplifying the denominator:

\[
\sqrt{(10)^2 - (2\sqrt{5})^2} = \sqrt{100 - 20} = \sqrt{80} = 4\sqrt{5}
\]

Thus, the expression simplifies to:

\[
\frac{4\pi \sqrt{10 + 2\sqrt{5}}}{4\sqrt{5}} = \frac{\pi \sqrt{10 + 2\sqrt{5}}}{\sqrt{5}}
\]

However, the more concise form is:

\[
\boxed{\frac{4\pi}{\sqrt{10 - 2\sqrt{5}}}}
\]
